```
    56→    print(
    57→        colorize(
    58→            "    Switch: `desloppify next --tier 1` | `desloppify next --tier 2` | "
    59→            "`desloppify next --tier 3` | `desloppify next --tier 4`",
    60→            "dim",
    61→        )
    62→    )
    63→
    64→
    65→def _render_grouped(items: list[dict], group: str) -> None:
    66→    grouped = group_queue_items(items, group)
    67→    for key, grouped_items in grouped.items():
    68→        print(colorize(f"\n  {key} ({len(grouped_items)})", "cyan"))
    69→        for item in grouped_items:
    70→            tier = int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3)))
    71→            tag = _effort_tag(item)
    72→            tag_str = f" {tag}" if tag else ""
    73→            print(
    74→                f"    {_tier_label(tier)} [{item.get('confidence', 'medium')}]{tag_str} {item.get('summary', '')}"
    75→            )
    76→
    77→
    78→def is_auto_fix_command(command: str | None) -> bool:
    79→    cmd = (command or "").strip()
    80→    return cmd.startswith("desloppify fix ") and "--dry-run" in cmd
    81→
    82→
    83→def _effort_tag(item: dict) -> str:
    84→    """Return a short effort/type tag for a queue item."""
    85→    if item.get("detector") == "review":
    86→        return "[review]"
    87→    if is_auto_fix_command(item.get("primary_command")):
    88→        return "[auto]"
    89→    return ""
    90→
    91→
    92→_ACTION_TYPE_LABELS = {
    93→    "auto_fix": "Auto-fixable batch",
    94→    "reorganize": "Reorganize batch",
    95→    "refactor": "Refactor batch",
    96→    "manual_fix": "Grouped task",
    97→}
    98→
    99→
   100→def _render_cluster_item(item: dict) -> None:
   101→    """Render an auto-cluster task card."""
   102→    member_count = int(item.get("member_count", 0))
   103→    action_type = item.get("action_type", "manual_fix")
   104→    cluster_name = item.get("id", "")
   105→    if cluster_name == "auto/initial-review":
   106→        type_label = "Initial subjective review"
   107→    elif cluster_name == "auto/stale-review":
   108→        type_label = "Stale subjective review"
   109→    else:
   110→        type_label = _ACTION_TYPE_LABELS.get(action_type, "Grouped task")
   111→    print(colorize(f"  ({type_label}, {member_count} findings)", "bold"))
   112→    print(colorize("  " + "─" * 60, "dim"))
   113→    print(f"  {colorize(item.get('summary', ''), 'yellow')}")
   114→
   115→    # Show breakdown by file (compact) + representative members
   116→    members = item.get("members", [])
   117→    if members:
   118→        # File distribution
   119→        from collections import Counter
   120→        file_counts = Counter(m.get("file", "?") for m in members)
   121→        if len(file_counts) <= 5:
   122→            print(colorize("\n  Files:", "dim"))
   123→            for f, c in file_counts.most_common():
   124→                print(f"    {f} ({c})")
   125→        else:
   126→            print(colorize(f"\n  Spread across {len(file_counts)} files:", "dim"))
   127→            for f, c in file_counts.most_common(3):
   128→                print(f"    {f} ({c})")
   129→            remaining = len(file_counts) - 3
   130→            print(colorize(f"    ... and {remaining} more files", "dim"))
   131→
   132→        # Sample IDs
   133→        print(colorize("\n  Sample:", "dim"))
   134→        for m in members[:3]:
   135→            print(f"    - {m.get('id', '')}")
   136→        if len(members) > 3:
   137→            print(colorize(f"    ... and {len(members) - 3} more", "dim"))
   138→
   139→    # Action
   140→    cluster_name = item.get("id", "")
   141→    primary_command = item.get("primary_command")
   142→    if primary_command:
   143→        print(colorize(f"\n  Action: {primary_command}", "cyan"))
   144→
   145→    # Resolution hints
   146→    print(colorize(f'\n  Resolve all:   desloppify plan done "{cluster_name}" '
   147→                   f'--note "<what>" --attest "{ATTEST_EXAMPLE}"', "dim"))
   148→    print(colorize(f"  Drill in:      desloppify next --cluster {cluster_name} --count 10",
   149→                   "dim"))
   150→    print(colorize(f"  Skip cluster:  desloppify plan skip {cluster_name}", "dim"))
   151→
   152→
   153→def _render_item(
   154→    item: dict, dim_scores: dict, findings_scoped: dict, explain: bool,
   155→    potentials: dict | None = None,
   156→) -> None:
   157→    # Delegate to cluster renderer for cluster meta-items
   158→    if item.get("kind") == "cluster":
   159→        _render_cluster_item(item)
   160→        return
   161→
   162→    tier = int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3)))
   163→    confidence = item.get("confidence", "medium")
   164→    print(colorize(f"  (Tier {tier}, {confidence} confidence)", "bold"))
   165→    print(colorize("  " + "─" * 60, "dim"))
   166→    print(f"  {colorize(item.get('summary', ''), 'yellow')}")
   167→
   168→    # Effort/type label
   169→    if item.get("detector") == "review":
   170→        print(colorize("  Type: Design review (requires judgment)", "dim"))
   171→    elif is_auto_fix_command(item.get("primary_command")):
   172→        print(colorize("  Type: Auto-fixable", "dim"))
   173→
   174→    kind = item.get("kind", "finding")
   175→    if kind == "subjective_dimension":
   176→        detail = item.get("detail", {})
   177→        subjective_score = float(
   178→            detail.get("strict_score", item.get("subjective_score", 100.0))
   179→        )
   180→        print(f"  Dimension: {detail.get('dimension_name', 'unknown')}")
   181→        print(f"  Score: {subjective_score:.1f}% (always queued as T4)")
   182→        print(
   183→            colorize(
   184→                f"  Action: {item.get('primary_command', 'desloppify review --prepare')}",
   185→                "cyan",
   186→            )
   187→        )
   188→        print(colorize(
   189→            "  Note: re-review scores what it finds — scores can go down if issues are discovered.",
   190→            "dim",
   191→        ))
   192→        if explain:
   193→            reason = item.get("explain", {}).get(
   194→                "policy",
   195→                "subjective items are fixed at T4 and do not outrank mechanical T1/T2/T3.",
   196→            )
   197→            print(colorize(f"  explain: {reason}", "dim"))
   198→        return
   199→
   200→    # Plan overrides: description, cluster, note
   201→    if item.get("plan_description"):
   202→        print(colorize(f"  → {item['plan_description']}", "cyan"))
   203→    plan_cluster = item.get("plan_cluster")
   204→    if isinstance(plan_cluster, dict):
   205→        cluster_name = plan_cluster.get("name", "")
   206→        cluster_desc = plan_cluster.get("description") or ""
   207→        total = plan_cluster.get("total_items", 0)
   208→        desc_str = f' — "{cluster_desc}"' if cluster_desc else ""
   209→        print(colorize(f"  Cluster: {cluster_name}{desc_str} ({total} items)", "dim"))
   210→    if item.get("plan_note"):
   211→        print(colorize(f"  Note: {item['plan_note']}", "dim"))
   212→
   213→    file = item.get("file", "")
   214→    if file and file != ".":
   215→        print(f"  File: {file}")
   216→    print(colorize(f"  ID:   {item.get('id', '')}", "dim"))
   217→
   218→    detail = item.get("detail", {})
   219→    if detail.get("lines"):
   220→        print(f"  Lines: {', '.join(str(line_no) for line_no in detail['lines'][:8])}")
   221→    if detail.get("category"):
   222→        print(f"  Category: {detail['category']}")
   223→    if detail.get("importers") is not None:
   224→        print(f"  Active importers: {detail['importers']}")
   225→    if detail.get("suggestion"):
   226→        print(colorize(f"\n  Suggestion: {detail['suggestion']}", "dim"))
   227→
   228→    target_line = detail.get("line") or (detail.get("lines", [None]) or [None])[0]
   229→    if target_line and item.get("file") not in (".", ""):
   230→        snippet = read_code_snippet(item["file"], target_line)
   231→        if snippet:
   232→            print(colorize("\n  Code:", "dim"))
   233→            print(snippet)
   234→
   235→    if dim_scores:
   236→        detector = item.get("detector", "")
   237→        dimension = scoring_mod.get_dimension_for_detector(detector)
   238→        if dimension and dimension.name in dim_scores:
   239→            dimension_score = dim_scores[dimension.name]
   240→            strict_val = dimension_score.get("strict", dimension_score["score"])
   241→            print(
   242→                colorize(
   243→                    f"\n  Dimension: {dimension.name} — {dimension_score['score']:.1f}% "
   244→                    f"(strict: {strict_val:.1f}%) "
   245→                    f"({dimension_score['issues']} of {dimension_score['checks']:,} checks failing)",
   246→                    "dim",
   247→                )
   248→            )
   249→
   250→    # Score impact estimate
   251→    detector = item.get("detector", "")
   252→    if potentials and detector and dim_scores:
   253→        try:
   254→            from desloppify.scoring import compute_score_impact
   255→            impact = compute_score_impact(dim_scores, potentials, detector, issues_to_fix=1)
   256→            if impact > 0:
   257→                print(colorize(f"  Impact: fixing this is worth ~+{impact:.1f} pts on overall score", "cyan"))
   258→            else:
   259→                # Single-item impact rounds to 0 — try bulk impact for all issues in detector
   260→                dimension = scoring_mod.get_dimension_for_detector(detector)
   261→                if dimension and dimension.name in dim_scores:
   262→                    issues = dim_scores[dimension.name].get("issues", 0)
   263→                    if issues > 1:
   264→                        bulk = compute_score_impact(dim_scores, potentials, detector, issues_to_fix=issues)
   265→                        if bulk > 0:
   266→                            print(colorize(
   267→                                f"  Impact: fixing all {issues} {detector} issues → ~+{bulk:.1f} pts",
   268→                                "cyan",
   269→                            ))
   270→        except (ImportError, TypeError, ValueError, KeyError):
   271→            pass
   272→    elif detector == "review" and dim_scores:
   273→        # Review findings: show dimension drag from breakdown
   274→        try:
   275→            from desloppify.scoring import compute_health_breakdown
   276→            dim_key = item.get("detail", {}).get("dimension", "")
   277→            if dim_key:
   278→                breakdown = compute_health_breakdown(dim_scores)
   279→                for entry in breakdown.get("entries", []):
   280→                    if not isinstance(entry, dict):
   281→                        continue
   282→                    entry_key = str(entry.get("name", "")).lower().replace(" ", "_")
   283→                    if entry_key == dim_key.lower().replace(" ", "_"):
   284→                        drag = float(entry.get("overall_drag", 0) or 0)
   285→                        if drag > 0.01:
   286→                            print(colorize(
   287→                                f"  Dimension drag: {entry['name']} costs -{drag:.2f} pts on overall score",
   288→                                "cyan",
   289→                            ))
   290→                        break
   291→        except (ImportError, TypeError, ValueError, KeyError):
   292→            pass
   293→
   294→    detector_name = item.get("detector", "")
   295→    auto_fix_command = item.get("primary_command")
   296→    if is_auto_fix_command(auto_fix_command):
   297→        similar_count = sum(
   298→            1
   299→            for finding in findings_scoped.values()
   300→            if finding.get("detector") == detector_name and finding["status"] == "open"
   301→        )
   302→        if similar_count > 1:
   303→            print(
   304→                colorize(
   305→                    f"\n  Auto-fixable: {similar_count} similar findings. "
   306→                    f"Run `{auto_fix_command}` to fix all at once.",
   307→                    "cyan",
   308→                )
   309→            )
   310→    if explain:
   311→        explanation = item.get("explain", {})
   312→        count_weight = explanation.get("count", int(detail.get("count", 0) or 0))
   313→        base = (
   314→            f"ranked by tier={tier}, confidence={confidence}, "
   315→            f"count={count_weight}, id={item.get('id', '')}"
   316→        )
   317→
   318→        # Add dimension context for mechanical detectors
   319→        if dim_scores and detector:
   320→            dimension = scoring_mod.get_dimension_for_detector(detector)
   321→            if dimension and dimension.name in dim_scores:
   322→                ds = dim_scores[dimension.name]
   323→                base += (
   324→                    f". Dimension: {dimension.name} at {ds['score']:.1f}% "
   325→                    f"({ds['issues']} open issues)"
   326→                )
   327→
   328→        # For review findings, show subjective dimension context
   329→        if item.get("detector") == "review" and dim_scores:
   330→            dim_key = item.get("detail", {}).get("dimension", "")
   331→            if dim_key:
   332→                for ds_name, ds_data in dim_scores.items():
   333→                    if ds_name.lower().replace(" ", "_") == dim_key.lower().replace(" ", "_"):
   334→                        score_val = ds_data.get("score", "?")
   335→                        score_str = f"{score_val:.1f}" if isinstance(score_val, (int, float)) else str(score_val)
   336→                        base += f". Subjective dimension: {ds_name} at {score_str}%"
   337→                        break
   338→
   339→        policy = explanation.get("policy")
   340→        if policy:
   341→            base = f"{base}. {policy}"
   342→        print(colorize(f"  explain: {base}", "dim"))
   343→
   344→
   345→def render_queue_header(queue: dict, explain: bool) -> None:
   346→    _render_tier_navigator(queue)
   347→    if not queue.get("fallback_reason"):
   348→        return
   349→    print(colorize(f"  {queue['fallback_reason']}", "yellow"))
   350→    if not explain:
   351→        return
   352→    available = queue.get("available_tiers", [])
   353→    if available:
   354→        tiers = ", ".join(f"T{tier_num}" for tier_num in available)
   355→        print(colorize(f"  explain: available tiers are {tiers}", "dim"))
   356→
   357→
   358→def show_empty_queue(queue: dict, tier: int | None, strict: float | None) -> bool:
   359→    if queue.get("items"):
   360→        return False
   361→    suffix = f" Strict score: {strict:.1f}/100" if strict is not None else ""
   362→    print(colorize(f"\n  Nothing to do!{suffix}", "green"))
   363→    if tier is not None:
   364→        print(colorize(f"  Requested tier: T{tier}", "dim"))
   365→        available = queue.get("available_tiers", [])
   366→        if available:
   367→            commands = " | ".join(
   368→                f"desloppify next --tier {tier_num}" for tier_num in available
   369→            )
   370→            print(colorize(f"  Try: {commands}", "dim"))
   371→    return True
   372→
   373→
   374→def _render_strategy_summary(plan: dict | None) -> None:
   375→    """Show the meta-plan strategy summary when epics exist."""
   376→    if not plan:
   377→        return
   378→    meta = plan.get("epic_synthesis_meta", {})
   379→    summary = meta.get("strategy_summary", "")
   380→    if not summary:
   381→        return
   382→    print(colorize("\n  Meta-plan:", "bold"))
   383→    for line in summary.strip().splitlines():
   384→        print(colorize(f"  {line}", "dim"))
   385→
   386→
   387→def _render_synthesis_item(item: dict) -> None:
   388→    """Render the synthesis::pending card."""
   389→    detail = item.get("detail", {}) if isinstance(item.get("detail"), dict) else {}
   390→    total = detail.get("total_review_findings", 0)
   391→    new = detail.get("new_since_last", 0)
   392→    resolved = detail.get("resolved_since_last", 0)
   393→    trigger = detail.get("trigger", "unknown")
   394→    epic_count = detail.get("epic_count", 0)
   395→    pending = detail.get("pending_epics", 0)
   396→    in_prog = detail.get("in_progress_epics", 0)
   397→    completed = detail.get("completed_epics", 0)
   398→
   399→    print(colorize("  PLAN — do not proceed until complete", "bold"))
   400→    print(colorize("  " + "─" * 60, "dim"))
   401→    print(f"  {total} review findings need integration into the meta-plan.")
   402→    print(f"  Trigger: {trigger}")
   403→    print()
   404→    if new:
   405→        print(f"  New findings since last synthesis: {new}")
   406→    if resolved:
   407→        print(f"  Resolved since last synthesis: {resolved}")
   408→    if epic_count:
   409→        print(f"  Current epics: {epic_count} ({pending} pending, {in_prog} in progress, {completed} completed)")
   410→    print()
   411→    print(colorize("  This is a planning step. Do NOT start fixing code until this is done.", "yellow"))
   412→    print()
   413→    print("  Steps:")
   414→    print("    1. `desloppify plan synthesize`  — see all findings that need organizing")
   415→    print("    2. Group by root cause into clusters (create, add, move, focus)")
   416→    print('    3. `desloppify plan synthesize --complete --strategy "..."`')
   417→    print("    4. `desloppify next`  — start implementation")
   418→    print()
   419→    print(colorize("  Forbidden during this phase:", "red"))
   420→    print("    - desloppify fix / resolve / editing code / plan skip")
   421→    print()
   422→    print(colorize("  View plan:", "dim"))
   423→    print(colorize("    desloppify plan queue   — items in priority order", "dim"))
   424→    print(colorize("    desloppify plan show    — plan status summary", "dim"))
   425→
   426→
   427→def _render_epic_item(item: dict) -> None:
   428→    """Render an epic card."""
   429→    detail = item.get("detail", {}) if isinstance(item.get("detail"), dict) else {}
   430→    direction = detail.get("direction", "")
   431→    thesis = detail.get("thesis", "")
   432→    root_cause = detail.get("root_cause", "")
   433→    status = detail.get("epic_status", "pending")
   434→    agent_safe = detail.get("agent_safe", False)
   435→    dep_order = detail.get("dependency_order", 999)
   436→    total_epics = detail.get("total_epics", 0)
   437→    finding_ids = detail.get("finding_ids", [])
   438→    action_steps = detail.get("action_steps", [])
   439→
   440→    safe_tag = " agent-safe" if agent_safe else ""
   441→    print(colorize(f"  [Epic — {direction}] ({len(finding_ids)} findings,{safe_tag} dependency_order: {dep_order})", "bold"))
   442→    print(colorize("  " + "─" * 60, "dim"))
   443→    print(f"  Thesis: {thesis}")
   444→    if root_cause:
   445→        print(f"  Root cause: {root_cause}")
   446→    print(f"  Status: {status}")
   447→    if total_epics:
   448→        print(f"  This is part of the meta-plan (epic {dep_order} of {total_epics}).")
   449→    print()
   450→    if action_steps:
   451→        print("  Steps:")
   452→        for i, step in enumerate(action_steps, 1):
   453→            print(f"    {i}. {step}")
   454→        print()
   455→    if finding_ids:
   456→        shown = finding_ids[:5]
   457→        print("  Findings in this epic:")
   458→        for fid in shown:
   459→            print(f"    - {fid}")
   460→        if len(finding_ids) > 5:
   461→            print(f"    ... and {len(finding_ids) - 5} more")
   462→        print()
   463→    epic_name = item.get("id", "")
   464→    print(f"  Work on this epic:")
   465→    print(f"    desloppify plan focus {epic_name}")
   466→    print(f"    (then work through findings with desloppify next)")
   467→
   468→
   469→def render_terminal_items(
   470→    items: list[dict],
   471→    dim_scores: dict,
   472→    findings_scoped: dict,
   473→    *,
   474→    group: str,
   475→    explain: bool,
   476→    potentials: dict | None = None,
   477→    plan: dict | None = None,
   478→) -> None:
   479→    # Show strategy summary when epics exist
   480→    _render_strategy_summary(plan)
   481→
   482→    # Show focus header if plan has active cluster
   483→    if plan and plan.get("active_cluster"):
   484→        cluster_name = plan["active_cluster"]
   485→        clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   486→        cluster_data = clusters.get(cluster_name, {})
   487→        total = len(cluster_data.get("finding_ids", []))
   488→        visible = sum(1 for it in items if it.get("kind") != "synthesis_needed")
   489→        print(colorize(f"\n  Focused on: {cluster_name} ({visible} of {total} remaining)", "cyan"))
   490→
   491→        # Exclude the synthesis item from the focus count — it's a queue-level
   492→        # blocker, not a cluster member, so don't count it in "N of M remaining".
   493→        focus_items = [it for it in items if it.get("kind") != "synthesis_needed"]
   494→        if len(focus_items) == 0:
   495→            print(colorize(
   496→                "  This cluster has no remaining items. "
   497→                "Run `desloppify plan focus --clear` to unfocus, "
   498→                "or `desloppify plan cluster show` to inspect.",
   499→                "yellow",
   500→            ))
   501→
   502→    # When synthesis is pending, only show the planning card — don't leak
   503→    # implementation tasks that would tempt agents to skip the planning step.
   504→    if items and items[0].get("kind") == "synthesis_needed":
   505→        queue_pos = items[0].get("queue_position")
   506→        pos_str = f"  (#{queue_pos} in queue)" if queue_pos else ""
   507→        print(colorize(f"  Next item{pos_str}", "bold"))
   508→        _render_synthesis_item(items[0])
   509→        return
   510→
   511→    if group != "item":
   512→        _render_grouped(items, group)
   513→        return
   514→    for idx, item in enumerate(items):
   515→        if idx > 0:
   516→            print()
   517→        kind = item.get("kind", "")
   518→        queue_pos = item.get("queue_position")
   519→
   520→        # All item types get the same position label — no visual hierarchy
   521→        # that makes synthetic items look like metadata instead of tasks.
   522→        if queue_pos and len(items) > 1:
   523→            label = f"  [#{queue_pos}]"
   524→        elif len(items) > 1:
   525→            label = f"  [{idx + 1}/{len(items)}]"
   526→        else:
   527→            pos_str = f"  (#{ queue_pos} in queue)" if queue_pos else ""
   528→            label = f"  Next item{pos_str}"
   529→        print(colorize(label, "bold"))
   530→
   531→        if kind == "epic":
   532→            _render_epic_item(item)
   533→            continue
   534→        _render_item(item, dim_scores, findings_scoped, explain=explain, potentials=potentials)
   535→
   536→
   537→def render_single_item_resolution_hint(items: list[dict]) -> None:
   538→    if len(items) != 1:
   539→        return
   540→    kind = items[0].get("kind", "")
   541→    if kind in ("cluster", "synthesis_needed", "epic"):
   542→        return  # These cards include their own resolution hints
   543→    if kind != "finding":
   544→        return
   545→    item = items[0]
   546→    detector_name = item.get("detector", "")
   547→    if detector_name == "subjective_review":
   548→        print(colorize("\n  Review with:", "dim"))
   549→        primary = item.get(
   550→            "primary_command", "desloppify show subjective"
   551→        )
   552→        print(f"    {primary}")
   553→        if is_holistic_subjective_finding(item):
   554→            print("    desloppify review --prepare")

   574→
   575→def render_followup_nudges(
   576→    state: dict,
   577→    dim_scores: dict,
   578→    findings_scoped: dict,
   579→    *,
   580→    strict_score: float | None,
   581→    target_strict_score: float,
   582→) -> None:
   583→    subjective_threshold = target_strict_score
   584→    subjective_entries = scorecard_subjective(state, dim_scores)
   585→    followup = build_subjective_followup(
   586→        state,
   587→        subjective_entries,
   588→        threshold=subjective_threshold,
   589→        max_quality_items=3,
   590→        max_integrity_items=5,
   591→    )
   592→    unassessed_subjective = unassessed_subjective_dimensions(dim_scores)
   593→    if strict_score is not None:
   594→        gap = round(float(target_strict_score) - float(strict_score), 1)
   595→        if gap > 0:
   596→            print(
   597→                colorize(
   598→                    f"\n  North star: strict {strict_score:.1f}/100 → target {target_strict_score:.1f} (+{gap:.1f} needed)",
   599→                    "cyan",
   600→                )
   601→            )
   602→        else:
   603→            print(
   604→                colorize(
   605→                    f"\n  North star: strict {strict_score:.1f}/100 meets target {target_strict_score:.1f}",
   606→                    "green",
   607→                )
   608→            )
   609→    # Integrity penalty/warn lines preserved (anti-gaming safeguard, must remain visible).
   610→    for style, message in followup.integrity_lines:
   611→        print(colorize(f"\n  {message}", style))
   612→
   613→    # Collapsed subjective summary.
   614→    coverage_open, _coverage_reasons, _holistic_reasons = subjective_coverage_breakdown(
   615→        findings_scoped
   616→    )
   617→    parts: list[str] = []
   618→    low_dims = len(followup.low_assessed)
   619→    unassessed_count = len(unassessed_subjective)
   620→    stale_count = sum(1 for e in subjective_entries if e.get("stale"))
   621→    open_review = [
   622→        f for f in findings_scoped.values()
   623→        if f.get("status") == "open" and f.get("detector") == "review"
   624→    ]
   625→    if low_dims:
   626→        parts.append(f"{low_dims} dimension{'s' if low_dims != 1 else ''} below target")
   627→    if stale_count:
   628→        parts.append(f"{stale_count} stale")
   629→    if unassessed_count:
   630→        parts.append(f"{unassessed_count} unassessed")
   631→    if len(open_review):
   632→        parts.append(f"{len(open_review)} review finding{'s' if len(open_review) != 1 else ''} open")
   633→    if coverage_open > 0:
   634→        parts.append(f"{coverage_open} file{'s' if coverage_open != 1 else ''} need review")
   635→
   636→    if parts:
   637→        print(colorize(f"\n  Subjective: {', '.join(parts)}.", "cyan"))
   638→        print(colorize("  Run `desloppify show subjective` for details.", "dim"))
   639→
   640→
   641→__all__ = [
   642→    "is_auto_fix_command",
   643→    "render_followup_nudges",
   644→    "render_queue_header",
   645→    "render_single_item_resolution_hint",
   646→    "render_terminal_items",

   648→    "show_empty_queue",
   649→    "subjective_coverage_breakdown",
   650→]
```

# Analysis: Multi-Stage Planning Gate for Agent Synthesis

## 1. The Core Problem, Restated Formally

The current system has a single state transition: `synthesis::pending → synthesis::complete`. The agent's obligation is to *understand the finding landscape and organize it into coherent work arcs*. But the system only checks the postcondition (clusters exist, findings are assigned) — it never checks whether the agent actually *understood* anything. The agent satisfies the postcondition mechanically and moves on.

This is a classic verification gap: you're checking the *artifact* (clusters exist) but not the *reasoning* (clusters reflect genuine root-cause analysis). Written reports are an attempt to make the reasoning observable and therefore verifiable.

Let me think through what this system actually needs.

---

## 2. What Cognitive Tasks Actually Need to Happen

Before proposing stages, let me enumerate what a competent human would do when looking at 30+ findings and needing to plan work:

1. **Read and internalize every finding.** Not skim — actually understand what each one is saying, in context.
2. **Notice patterns.** "These 7 findings are all symptoms of the same underlying problem."
3. **Notice contradictions.** "Finding #12 says extract a module, Finding #18 says inline the same module."
4. **Group by root cause.** Not by file, not by dimension — by the underlying architectural issue.
5. **Identify dependencies.** "We can't fix the type safety issues until we fix the module boundary issues, because the type exports cross the wrong boundaries."
6. **Sequence the work.** Dependencies create a partial order. Within that, prioritize by impact.
7. **Articulate a strategy.** "Here's the narrative arc: first stabilize the foundation, then restructure, then polish."

The key insight: **steps 1-3 are analytical** (understanding what you have), **steps 4-5 are organizational** (structuring what you understood), and **steps 6-7 are strategic** (deciding what to do about it).

The proposed 5 stages (assess, group, conflicts, order, strategy) roughly map to this, but I have opinions about the decomposition.

---

## 3. My Proposed Stages

I would use **four stages**, not five. Here's why:

### Stage 1: SURVEY

**Cognitive task:** Read every finding, restate each one in your own words, identify what's surprising or non-obvious.

**Why this exists:** The #1 failure mode is agents not actually reading the findings. If you force them to restate findings in their own words, they have to process them. The "what's surprising" question forces engagement beyond mechanical restating.

**Report requirements:**
- For each finding: a one-sentence restatement in different words than the finding itself (the tool can do fuzzy similarity checking — reject if >80% token overlap with the original finding text)
- At the end: "The most surprising finding is X because Y" and "The finding I'd be most tempted to ignore is Z because W"
- Minimum: 50 characters per finding + 200 characters for the summary section

**Mechanical preconditions to enter:** synthesis::pending is set, ≥3 findings exist
**Mechanical preconditions to complete:** report covers every finding by ID, minimum lengths met
**Postcondition invariant:** every finding ID in the plan is referenced in the survey report

**Good report example:**
> **F-014 (naming/consistency):** The ReviewBatch class uses "batch" terminology but the surrounding code calls the same concept a "chunk" — this isn't just naming, it's a concept mismatch that means readers build the wrong mental model.
> 
> **Most surprising:** F-022 — I expected the duplicate validation logic in scan_workflow.py to be a simple extract-and-share refactor, but the two copies actually have subtly different semantics (one treats empty lists as "match all", the other as "match none"). This isn't just duplication, it's a latent bug.
>
> **Most tempting to ignore:** F-009 — the overly-broad exception handler in batch execution. It's there for resilience and touching it risks breaking the fault tolerance story. But the finding is right that it swallows actionable errors.

**Bad report example (would be rejected):**
> **F-014:** naming consistency issue in ReviewBatch class
> **F-022:** duplicate validation logic should be extracted
> **Most surprising:** F-022 because duplication is bad
> **Most tempting to ignore:** F-009 because it's about error handling

The bad version just compresses the original finding into fewer words. It shows no evidence of understanding.

**What the tool checks mechanically:**
- Every finding ID appears in the report
- Per-finding text meets minimum character count (50 chars)
- Per-finding text has <80% token overlap with original finding description (prevents copy-paste with minor edits)
- Summary section ("surprising" and "tempting to ignore") each meet 200 char minimum
- Both required summary prompts are answered

---

### Stage 2: GROUP

**Cognitive task:** Identify root causes. Cluster findings by underlying issue, not surface category. Explicitly name contradictions.

**Why this exists:** This is where the actual analytical work happens. Grouping by root cause is the hardest cognitive task and the one agents most consistently fail at. Agents default to grouping by file, directory, or dimension name — all surface features. 

**Report requirements:**
- For each proposed cluster: a root cause statement (not a category label — a causal explanation of WHY these findings exist)
- For each finding placed in a cluster: one sentence explaining why this finding is a symptom of that root cause, not just co-located with it
- If any two findings appear to contradict each other: explicit acknowledgment and explanation of how the cluster strategy resolves the contradiction
- Minimum: 100 characters per cluster root cause + 40 characters per finding-to-cluster justification

**Mechanical preconditions to enter:** Stage 1 (SURVEY) complete
**Mechanical preconditions to complete:** 
- All findings assigned to a cluster
- No single cluster contains >60% of findings (prevents "dump everything in one bucket" — if you genuinely have one root cause, you need a waiver in the report explaining why)
- At least 2 clusters exist
- Report covers every cluster, every finding-cluster assignment

**Good report example:**
> **Cluster: "Module boundary confusion between review and scan"**
> Root cause: The review subsystem and scan subsystem evolved independently and now share types by reaching into each other's internals rather than through a defined interface. This creates coupling where changes to scan internals break review, and vice versa.
>
> - F-014 (naming/consistency): The ReviewBatch/chunk terminology mismatch exists because review borrowed scan's internal "chunk" concept but renamed it without updating the interface contract.
> - F-022 (duplication): The duplicate validation logic exists because both subsystems needed the same check but had no shared location to put it — they couldn't import from each other without circular deps, so they copied.
> - F-031 (complexity): The convoluted type narrowing in review_merge.py exists because it receives scan-internal types it shouldn't see, and has to defensively handle shapes that "shouldn't" occur.

> **Contradiction noted:** F-017 recommends extracting a shared validation module, while F-029 recommends making scan fully self-contained. These pull in opposite directions. Resolution: F-017 is the right direction — the shared module should live in `core/` as a neutral dependency. F-029's self-containment goal is served by removing scan's dependency on review internals, not by duplicating shared logic.

**Bad report example:**
> **Cluster: "Review issues"**
> Root cause: Various issues in the review subsystem.
> - F-014: It's about review code
> - F-022: It's about validation in review
> - F-031: It's in review_merge.py

This is grouping by directory, not by root cause. The "root cause" is just restating the category. The per-finding justifications are tautological.

**What the tool checks mechanically:**
- Every finding is in exactly one cluster
- No cluster has >60% of findings (soft limit — can override with explanation)
- Cluster root cause text meets minimum length
- Per-finding justification meets minimum length
- At least one contradiction section exists if there are findings with opposing direction tags, OR an explicit "no contradictions found because..." statement
- Clusters actually exist in the plan (CLI commands were run, not just described in prose)

---

### Stage 3: ORDER

**Cognitive task:** Establish dependencies between clusters and sequence them. Every ordering decision must have a stated reason.

**Why this exists:** Agents assign random priorities or alphabetical order. Sequencing requires understanding how changes interact — which work creates preconditions for other work, which work is safe to parallelize, what the critical path is.

I'm merging the originally-proposed "conflicts" and "order" stages. Here's why: conflicts and ordering are the same cognitive act. You discover ordering constraints BY identifying conflicts and dependencies. Separating them creates a stage ("conflicts") that's purely analytical with no organizational output, followed by a stage ("order") that needs to redo the analytical work anyway. One combined stage with one report is better.

**Report requirements:**
- A dependency graph: for each cluster, list which clusters must come before it and why
- For each dependency edge: one sentence explaining the mechanism (what specifically breaks if you do B before A)
- Clusters with no dependencies must be explicitly marked as "independent — safe to do in any order" with explanation of why
- The final proposed sequence, as an ordered list
- For adjacent items in the sequence: why this one before the next one (dependency, risk, impact — the reason must be stated)
- Minimum: 80 characters per dependency justification, 60 characters per adjacency justification

**Mechanical preconditions to enter:** Stage 2 (GROUP) complete, clusters exist in plan
**Mechanical preconditions to complete:**
- Plan queue order has been set (CLI move commands were issued)
- Every cluster in the plan has a priority position
- Report covers every cluster-pair dependency
- The stated sequence in the report matches the actual plan queue order

**Good report example:**
> **Dependencies:**
> - "Module boundary confusion" BEFORE "Type safety gaps": The type safety fixes require importing shared types from the new module boundary. If we fix types first, we'll wire them to the wrong interfaces and have to redo the work.
> - "Module boundary confusion" BEFORE "Error handling cleanup": The overly-broad exception handlers exist partly because the boundary confusion makes it unclear which exceptions can actually occur. Once boundaries are clean, the exception sets become obvious.
> - "Error handling cleanup" is INDEPENDENT of "Type safety gaps": These touch different code paths and neither creates preconditions for the other. Could be parallelized.
>
> **Sequence:**
> 1. Module boundary confusion — foundation, unblocks everything else
> 2. Type safety gaps — depends on clean boundaries; high impact because it prevents a class of bugs
> 3. Error handling cleanup — depends on clean boundaries but not on types; lower urgency, higher risk of regression if done wrong
> 4. Documentation drift — independent, low risk, can fill gaps between the above

**Bad report example:**
> **Sequence:**
> 1. Type safety gaps — most findings
> 2. Module boundary confusion — important
> 3. Error handling cleanup — needs fixing
> 4. Documentation drift — easy

No dependency reasoning. Ordering by finding count is not a strategy. "Important" and "needs fixing" are not justifications.

**What the tool checks mechanically:**
- Plan queue has been reordered (not default order)
- Every cluster appears in the report's dependency analysis
- Stated sequence matches actual plan queue order (no lying about what you did)
- Minimum lengths on justifications

---

### Stage 4: COMMIT

**Cognitive task:** Write the strategy narrative that ties everything together. This is the "letter to the executing agent" — the thing that will be read when actual code changes begin.

I'm renaming "strategy" to "commit" for a reason: this is the agent committing to a plan. The word matters. It's not an abstract strategy document — it's a contract.

**Report requirements:**
- One-paragraph summary of the overall situation (what's wrong with this codebase at a structural level)
- The execution plan: for each cluster, in sequence order, what the agent will actually DO (not "fix issues" — specific architectural moves)
- Risk callouts: for each cluster, what could go wrong during execution, and what the agent should watch for
- Success criteria: how will we know each cluster is done? Not "findings are resolved" — what structural property will hold?
- A "stop condition": under what circumstances should the agent STOP executing and re-plan? (e.g., "if fixing module boundaries reveals that the review subsystem needs a fundamentally different architecture, stop and re-survey")
- Minimum: 300 characters for situation summary, 150 characters per cluster execution plan, 100 characters per risk callout, 100 characters per success criterion, 200 characters for stop condition

**Mechanical preconditions to enter:** Stage 3 (ORDER) complete
**Mechanical preconditions to complete:**
- Report covers every cluster
- All minimum lengths met
- All required sections present

**Good strategy excerpt:**
> **Cluster 1: Module boundary confusion**
> Execution: Create `core/review_scan_bridge.py` as the single interface between review and scan. Move shared types (ValidationSpec, ChunkDescriptor) there. Update all cross-boundary imports to go through the bridge. Delete the duplicated validation logic and replace both call sites with the shared version from the bridge.
> Risk: The "duplicate" validation copies have diverged semantically (see survey note on F-022). Need to decide which semantic is correct before merging. If the answer is "both are correct for their context," we need two named variants in the bridge, not one.
> Success: No file in `review/` imports from `scan/` internals. No file in `scan/` imports from `review/` internals. All cross-boundary communication goes through the bridge module. Zero duplicate logic.
> 
> **Stop condition:** If during boundary cleanup we discover more than 5 types that need to cross the boundary, the subsystems are more entangled than this plan assumes. Stop, re-survey with the new understanding, and re-group.

**Bad strategy excerpt:**
> **Cluster 1: Module boundary confusion**
> Execution: Fix the module boundaries to be cleaner.
> Risk: Might break things.
> Success: Module boundaries are clean.

---

## 4. State Machine and Transitions

```
                    ┌──────────────────┐
                    │  synthesis::off   │ (no synthesis needed, <3 findings)
                    └──────────────────┘
                             │
                    (findings accumulate)
                             │
                             ▼
                    ┌──────────────────┐
         ┌────────▶│ synthesis::survey │◀────────────────────┐
         │         └──────────────────┘                      │
         │                   │                               │
         │          (submit survey report)                   │
         │                   │                               │
         │                   ▼                               │
         │         ┌──────────────────┐                      │
         │         │ synthesis::group  │                      │
         │         └──────────────────┘                 (landscape_changed)
         │                   │                               │
         │          (submit group report,                    │
         │           clusters created)                       │
         │                   │                               │
         │                   ▼                               │
         │         ┌──────────────────┐                      │
         │         │ synthesis::order  │──────────────────────┘
         │         └──────────────────┘
         │                   │
         │          (submit order report,
         │           queue reordered)
         │                   │
         │                   ▼
         │         ┌──────────────────┐
         │         │synthesis::commit  │──────────────────────┐
         │         └──────────────────┘                      │
         │                   │                          (landscape_changed)
         │          (submit commit report)                   │
         │                   │                               │
         │                   ▼                               │
         │         ┌──────────────────┐                      │
         │         │synthesis::active  │◀─────────(re-trigger)│
         │         └──────────────────┘                      │
         │                   │                               │
         │          (all clusters complete)                  │
         │                   │                               │
         │                   ▼                               │
         │         ┌──────────────────┐                      │
         └─────────│  synthesis::off   │                     │
                   └──────────────────┘                      │
                                                             │
                   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────┘
                   │ landscape_changed triggers:
                   │  - New scan adds ≥3 findings not covered by existing clusters
                   │  - ≥30% of clustered findings are resolved (plan is stale)
                   │  - Finding is superseded that was a cluster's justification
                   └─────────────────────────────────────────
```

### Key Transition Rules

**Forward transitions** require:
1. Current stage preconditions met (mechanical checks pass)
2. Report submitted and validated (length, coverage, no-copy checks)
3. No landscape change detected since entering current stage

**Backward transitions** (resets):
- `landscape_changed` resets to `synthesis::survey` from any stage EXCEPT:
  - If in `synthesis::survey`, it just invalidates the current report (must re-do)
  - If in `synthesis::active` (execution), it resets to `synthesis::survey` (full re-plan)
- An agent can voluntarily reset to any earlier stage (e.g., "I realize my grouping was wrong, let me redo from GROUP"). This is *good behavior* and should be easy.

**Landscape change detection:**
After each scan, compare:
- New findings not present in any cluster: count > 2 → landscape changed
- Resolved findings that were in clusters: if >30% of any cluster's findings are resolved → landscape changed
- Any finding marked superseded → check if it was the "anchor" finding of a cluster (the one the root cause statement primarily references)

**Important invariant:** Reports are append-only within a planning cycle. When you complete SURVEY and move to GROUP, the survey report is frozen and visible. The GROUP report can reference the SURVEY report. This creates a chain of reasoning the executing agent can read.

---

## 5. Minimum Character Counts — Rationale

I've been specific above, but let me consolidate and justify:

| Context | Minimum | Rationale |
|---------|---------|-----------|
| Per-finding restatement (SURVEY) | 50 chars | ~10 words. Enough for a genuine restatement, too short for a paragraph. Forces compression of understanding. |
| Survey summary questions | 200 chars each | ~40 words. Enough to require a real "because" clause. |
| Cluster root cause (GROUP) | 100 chars | ~20 words. A real causal statement needs this much. "Various issues in X" is ~25 chars — too short. |
| Finding-to-cluster justification | 40 chars | ~8 words. Brief but must be more than "it's in this file." |
| Dependency justification (ORDER) | 80 chars | ~16 words. Must explain the mechanism, not just assert. |
| Adjacency justification (ORDER) | 60 chars | ~12 words. Must state the reason for this-before-that. |
| Situation summary (COMMIT) | 300 chars | ~60 words. A real paragraph. |
| Per-cluster execution plan | 150 chars | ~30 words. Must describe specific actions. |
| Risk callout per cluster | 100 chars | ~20 words. Must identify the specific risk. |
| Success criterion per cluster | 100 chars | ~20 words. Must be a testable property. |
| Stop condition | 200 chars | ~40 words. Must describe a concrete trigger. |

**These are floors, not targets.** A good report will exceed them substantially. The purpose is to make it mechanically impossible to submit a one-liner for any section.

---

## 6. Failure Modes of This System

This is the most important section. Let me be honest about how agents will try to game this.

### Failure Mode 1: Verbose Bullshit
**Attack:** Agent meets character counts by being verbose and repetitive rather than insightful. "The module boundary confusion root cause is that the module boundaries are confused, which causes confusion about where modules begin and end, leading to a state of boundary confusion."

**Mitigation:** This is hard to fully prevent mechanically. Partial mitigations:
- The token-overlap check between survey restatements and original findings helps for SURVEY
- For other stages: consider a compression ratio check. If you can compress the text to <40% of its length without losing information (using a lightweight LLM call), the text was padded. BUT this adds an LLM dependency to the validation step, which has its own failure modes.
- **Pragmatic take:** Accept that some agents will produce verbose mediocre reports. The act of writing ANYTHING forces more engagement than writing nothing. Even a mediocre report with verbose root-cause statements is better than no planning. Don't let perfect be the enemy of good.

### Failure Mode 2: Templatic Responses
**Attack:** Agent learns the template and fills it in with minimally-adapted boilerplate. Every cluster gets "Root cause: insufficient abstraction in the X subsystem" with minor word substitutions.

**Mitigation:** 
- Cross-cluster similarity check: if any two cluster root cause statements have >70% token overlap, reject. Root causes should be distinct — that's the whole point of having multiple clusters.
- The "contradiction" requirement in GROUP forces engagement with specific content that can't be templated.

### Failure Mode 3: Stage-Skipping via File Manipulation
**Attack:** Agent directly edits plan.json to set the stage to `synthesis::active`, bypassing all gates.

**Mitigation:** 
- Reports must be stored as part of the plan state. The transition validator checks not just the stage flag but the existence and validity of the report for each completed stage.
- Add a hash chain: each stage's report includes a hash of the previous stage's report. If any stage is missing from the chain, the state is invalid.
- **Even better:** The plan file stores a `synthesis_reports` dict keyed by stage name. The transition function checks that all prior stages have valid reports. An agent that sets the stage to `active` without reports will hit a validation error on the next CLI command that reads plan state.

### Failure Mode 4: "What If There Are Only 3 Findings?"
**Edge case:** The system triggers synthesis for 3 findings. A 4-stage process with extensive reports is absurd for 3 findings.

**Mitigation:** Scale requirements by finding count.
- 3-5 findings: SURVEY and GROUP only (skip ORDER and COMMIT). The ordering is obvious with 2 clusters. Write a brief strategy as part of GROUP.
- 6-15 findings: Full 4-stage process with standard minimums.
- 16+ findings: Full process with higher minimums (larger landscape needs more analysis).

The thresholds should be in constants, not hardcoded in logic.

### Failure Mode 5: Perpetual Planning
**Attack (unintentional):** Agent gets stuck in planning loop because landscape keeps changing. Every scan adds findings, every addition triggers a reset, agent never reaches execution.

**Mitigation:**
- Landscape change detection should have a dampening mechanism. After a reset, the next reset requires a LARGER change (e.g., first reset at 3 new findings, second at 6, third at 10).
- Maximum resets per cycle: 3. After 3 resets, the system warns but allows proceeding with the current plan even if the landscape has shifted.
- The stop condition in the COMMIT report is the agent's own pre-commitment to when re-planning is warranted. Honor it.

### Failure Mode 6: Agent Refuses to Engage
**Attack:** Agent hits the planning gate and decides to work on something else entirely, or asks the user to skip it.

**Mitigation:** This is a UX problem, not a verification problem. The planning gate should:
- Clearly explain WHY it exists (in the CLI output, not just docs)
- Estimate time cost ("this typically takes 5-10 minutes for this many findings")
- Show the consequences of skipping ("without planning, the next 3 fix attempts are likely to conflict")
- Make each stage feel productive, not bureaucratic. The survey stage should reveal insights. The group stage should feel like solving a puzzle. The order stage should feel like strategic thinking.

---

## 7. Stages I'd Add or Remove

**Removed: Separate "conflicts" stage.** Merged into ORDER. Conflict detection without ordering is analysis without action. The ordering stage naturally requires you to identify conflicts (they manifest as dependency edges). Separating them creates busywork.

**Considered and rejected: "Validate" stage after COMMIT.** I thought about requiring the agent to re-read its own plan and check it for internal consistency. But this is the kind of meta-cognitive task that agents are worst at — they'll just say "looks good" every time. The validation is better done mechanically (the tool checks invariants) than via self-report.

**Considered and rejected: "Review" stage where a different agent checks the plan.** Multi-agent review is powerful but out of scope for this system. Worth considering as a future extension.

**What I'd add: A "findings I'm deliberately deferring" section in SURVEY.** Not every finding needs to be in the current planning cycle. The agent should be able to explicitly say "F-023 is real but not worth addressing now because X" and have those findings excluded from GROUP's coverage requirement. Without this, agents will create junk clusters to satisfy the "every finding must be clustered" invariant. The explicit deferral with stated reason is more honest and more useful.

---

## 8. Implementation Sketch

The synthesis stage should be stored in the plan schema, alongside the existing fields:

```python
class SynthesisState(TypedDict, total=False):
    stage: str  # "survey" | "group" | "order" | "commit" | "active" | "off"
    reports: dict[str, SynthesisReport]  # keyed by stage name
    report_chain: list[str]  # ordered list of stage names with completed reports
    reset_count: int
    finding_snapshot: list[str]  # finding IDs at synthesis start
    deferred_findings: dict[str, str]  # finding_id → reason
    
class SynthesisReport(TypedDict):
    stage: str
    text: str
    submitted_at: str  # ISO timestamp
    finding_coverage: list[str]  # finding IDs covered
    prev_report_hash: str  # hash of previous stage's report text
```

Each `plan synthesize <stage>` subcommand:
1. Checks that the current stage matches the requested stage
2. Validates mechanical preconditions
3. Accepts the report text
4. Validates report (length, coverage, similarity checks)
5. Stores the report
6. Advances the stage

The CLI surface would be:
```
plan synthesize survey --report "..."
plan synthesize group --report "..."
plan synthesize order --report "..."  
plan synthesize commit --report "..."
plan synthesize status  # shows current stage and what's needed
plan synthesize defer F-023 --reason "..."  # defer a finding
plan synthesize reset [stage]  # voluntary reset to earlier stage
```

---

## 9. The Deepest Problem

I want to end with a philosophical observation. The fundamental tension here is: **you're asking a system to verify that another system has genuinely thought about something.** This is, in the general case, impossible. You can't distinguish a thoughtful 200-character statement from a sophisticated-sounding but vacuous 200-character statement through mechanical checks alone.

What you CAN do is:
1. **Raise the floor.** Make it impossible to pass with zero effort. Character counts, coverage checks, and similarity detection do this.
2. **Create artifacts that are useful regardless.** Even if the survey report is mediocre, it's still a document the executing agent can reference. Even a so-so root cause statement is better than no root cause statement. The reports have value as communication artifacts even when they fail as proof-of-understanding.
3. **Make the right thing easier than the wrong thing.** If the stage structure mirrors how humans actually think about planning, agents will partially engage with it correctly just by following the structure. The stages should be designed so that even a mechanical traversal produces a passable plan.
4. **Trust but verify at execution time.** The real test of the plan is whether execution goes well. If an agent's plan leads to thrashing during execution, that's signal for the next planning cycle. Consider tracking plan quality metrics: how often does execution match the plan's predictions?

The multi-stage approach won't produce perfect plans. It will produce plans that are meaningfully better than no plans, with a floor that prevents the worst failure modes. That's the realistic goal, and it's worth pursuing.

## Analysis: Multi-Stage Planning for Desloppify

### Understanding What Exists

Having read through the codebase, I want to ground this in what's actually happening. The current flow is:

1. `sync_synthesis_needed()` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/stale_dimensions.py` detects that review findings have changed and injects `synthesis::pending` at the front of the queue.

2. `_render_synthesis_item()` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/next_parts/render.py` (line 387) shows a hard gate: "PLAN -- do not proceed until complete." It refuses to show implementation tasks behind it.

3. The agent runs `plan synthesize`, sees a list of findings, creates clusters with CLI commands, then runs `plan synthesize --complete --strategy "..."`.

4. `_cmd_synthesize_complete()` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/synthesize_handlers.py` checks that clusters exist with findings assigned, requires a `--strategy` string, and removes the blocker.

The existing system already has an LLM synthesis path (`synthesize_epics()` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/epic_synthesis.py`) with a well-designed prompt (`_SYNTHESIS_SYSTEM_PROMPT`), but the manual path is what agents actually use — and that's where they go through the motions.

### The Core Critique of the 5-Stage Proposal

The five-stage proposal (assess, group, conflicts, order, strategy) has a structural problem: **it linearizes a cognitive process that is naturally iterative, and it substitutes documentation for thinking.**

When I look at the existing `_SYNTHESIS_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, it already asks the LLM for exactly the right things: root-cause clustering, contradiction resolution, dependency ordering, dismissal rationale, priority reasoning. The problem isn't that the LLM doesn't know what good planning looks like. The problem is that the *manual* synthesis path presents findings as a flat list and says "now organize these," which invites shallow pattern-matching.

Five stages with character minimums will produce five paragraphs of text that sound like planning but aren't. I've seen this pattern in every organization that tries to solve "people aren't thinking deeply enough" with "add more forms." The forms get filled out. The thinking doesn't improve. The overhead increases. The agents that were already planning well now spend twice as long documenting what they would have done anyway, and the agents that were skimming now produce more words of skimming.

### What Actually Causes Shallow Planning

The failure modes listed in the problem statement are real, but they have a common root: **the agent never has to prove it read the findings carefully, and the output format doesn't make shallow work visible.**

Currently, the agent sees a list like:
```
[naming] utils.py has inconsistent naming
  review-abc123
[naming] helpers.py has inconsistent naming
  review-def456
[structure] Too many responsibilities in engine.py
  review-ghi789
```

And creates clusters like:
```
desloppify plan cluster create naming-cleanup --description "Fix naming issues"
desloppify plan cluster add naming-cleanup review-abc123 review-def456
```

This is keyword matching, not root-cause analysis. But the tool has no way to distinguish "I clustered these because they're both about naming in modules that share an import chain, and fixing one will change the API the other depends on" from "I clustered these because they both say 'naming'."

### What I'd Actually Design

I would not build five stages. I would build **two phases with different cognitive modes**, plus a **specificity check** that makes shallow work fail mechanically.

#### Phase 1: Observation (reading mode)

The tool presents findings not as a flat list, but in a way that makes patterns and tensions visible. This means:

- **Group findings by file** first, showing which files have multiple findings (these are where root causes live)
- **Show finding pairs that reference the same code** or the same abstractions
- **Highlight potential contradictions** — findings where one says "extract" and another says "inline" about related code, or where one says "too many abstractions" and another says "missing abstraction layer"
- **Show the dependency graph** — which files import which, so the agent can see how changes cascade

The output of this phase is a single structured artifact: the **observation report**. But here's the key design choice — the report is not freeform prose. It's a structured format with **specific referents**:

```
desloppify plan synthesize --observe
```

This produces a structured view and then asks the agent to fill in an observation template:

```
desloppify plan synthesize --observations '{
  "tensions": [
    {
      "finding_a": "review-abc123",
      "finding_b": "review-ghi789",
      "nature": "These two findings pull in opposite directions: abc123 wants to simplify the naming in utils.py, but ghi789 wants to decompose engine.py, which would create new modules that need those same names as public API."
    }
  ],
  "root_causes": [
    {
      "description": "The real problem is that engine.py grew into a god module because utils.py\'s inconsistent naming made it hard to extract stable interfaces. The naming issues and the structural issues share a root cause: no clear module boundary contract.",
      "evidence": ["review-abc123", "review-def456", "review-ghi789"]
    }
  ],
  "filing_notes": [
    {
      "finding_id": "review-xyz999",
      "note": "This is a false positive — the apparent duplication is intentional because the two functions handle different error recovery strategies."
    }
  ]
}'
```

The critical design element: **every claim must cite specific finding IDs, and the tool validates that the cited IDs exist and are relevant.** You can't write "some findings are contradictory" — you have to say *which* findings, and the tool checks that those findings exist in the open set.

The specificity requirement is the real guard against shallow work. It's not a character minimum — it's a *reference* minimum. The observation must mention at least N% of the open findings by ID, and each tension or root cause must cite at least 2 specific findings. This is something the tool can verify mechanically, and it directly correlates with "the agent actually read the findings" in a way that character count does not.

#### Phase 2: Structuring (organizing mode)

Only after observations are submitted does the tool allow clustering. And now the clustering commands are enriched: when the agent creates a cluster, the tool checks whether the cluster's members were mentioned together in the observation report. If they weren't, it warns: "These findings weren't identified as sharing a root cause in your observations. Add a root_cause note connecting them, or proceed with --force."

This isn't a hard gate — sometimes you realize connections during structuring that you didn't see during observation. But the warning creates a natural moment of "wait, did I actually think about why these go together?"

The completion step (`--complete`) then requires:

1. A strategy that references specific clusters by name (not generic "we'll improve code quality")
2. A dependency rationale that explains why cluster A must come before cluster B by referencing what A produces that B needs
3. Every finding must be either clustered or explicitly dismissed with a reason

The tool validates that the strategy mentions each cluster name, that the dependency rationale references at least two clusters, and that all findings are accounted for. These are mechanical checks, but they're checks that select for specificity rather than verbosity.

### Why Two Phases, Not Five

The observation/structuring split maps to the real cognitive boundary: **reading vs. writing**. When you're observing, you should be in receptive mode — noticing, connecting, wondering. When you're structuring, you should be in decisive mode — committing, ordering, justifying.

Five stages fragment both modes into pieces too small for either to develop. You can't genuinely assess findings in isolation from grouping them — the assessment IS the grouping, mentally. You can't order without understanding conflicts, because conflicts determine dependencies. The stages aren't independent; they're aspects of a single integrative act.

Two phases, by contrast, respect the natural grain: first understand, then decide. And the boundary between them is meaningful — the observation report is a genuine artifact that the structuring phase refers back to, not just a waypoint on a progress bar.

### The Audience Problem

Who reads these reports? This is the question that determines whether the system is performative or functional.

**The agent itself during execution.** This is the highest-value audience. When the agent is fixing code in cluster 3 and wonders "wait, does this conflict with what cluster 1 was supposed to do?", it should be able to look at the observation report and find the answer. This means the report needs to be *queryable* — stored as structured data, not prose.

**Future synthesis runs.** When findings change and synthesis re-triggers, the next synthesis should see what the previous one decided and why. The existing `epic_synthesis_meta` structure in the schema already supports this (the `strategy_summary` field), but observations and tension notes would make re-synthesis much more informed.

**Human reviewers, occasionally.** A human might look at the plan to understand what the agent decided. But this is the least important audience — if the plan works well, the human doesn't need to audit it. Design for the machine audience (the agent) and the human gets readability as a side effect.

The implication: **store observations as structured data in the plan, not as freeform text in reports**. The `PlanModel` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/schema.py` should grow an `observations` field — a list of tensions, root causes, and filing notes, each with finding ID references. This data is machine-readable, human-skimmable, and actually useful during execution.

### Making the Agent Want to Plan Well

This is the hardest question, and I want to be honest that I don't have a complete answer.

The multi-stage approach tries to force quality through process. But the best quality comes from intrinsic motivation — the feeling that the planning is genuinely helping you do better work. Here are some design moves that might create that feeling:

1. **Show the agent what bad planning costs.** After execution, if the agent had to backtrack or undo work because of a planning miss, surface that: "Finding X was resolved, then reopened because it conflicted with finding Y, which was in a different cluster. If these had been in the same cluster, this backtrack would have been avoided." This creates a feedback loop where good planning has visible payoff.

2. **Make the observation phase produce surprise.** If the tool can detect potential contradictions or unusual patterns in the findings and surface them ("Did you notice that findings A and B reference the same function but recommend opposite changes?"), the observation phase becomes genuinely interesting rather than rote. The agent isn't just documenting what it already knows — it's being shown things it might not have noticed.

3. **Celebrate specificity, not length.** When the agent submits observations, the tool should respond with "Your observations reference 14 of 16 open findings and identify 3 tensions" — quantifying the coverage rather than the word count. This makes thoroughness feel like competence rather than compliance.

4. **Make the plan a living artifact the agent refers back to.** During execution, when the agent runs `next` and gets a finding, show the observation note that was filed about that finding. Show which cluster it's in and why. Make the planning work *visible* in the execution flow, so the agent experiences the planning as scaffolding for its own work rather than a hoop it jumped through.

### Honest Tensions and Tradeoffs

**Tension: Specificity checks can be gamed too.** An agent can cite finding IDs without understanding them. "review-abc123 and review-def456 share a root cause: they are both about code quality in this module" is specific but vacuous. There's no mechanical check that can fully distinguish genuine insight from sophisticated-looking compliance.

**Tension: Two phases might not be enough.** Some codebases have 50+ review findings where the observation phase alone is a significant cognitive load. Forcing it all into one report might be as overwhelming as a flat list. But splitting it into sub-phases risks the bureaucracy problem again.

**Tension: Structured data vs. narrative reasoning.** I've argued for structured observations (JSON with finding IDs) rather than prose reports. But the best planning reasoning is narrative — it involves telling a *story* about what's wrong with the codebase and what the fix looks like. A JSON format might suppress the narrative mode that produces genuine insight. The right answer might be structured data *plus* a narrative field, but that's getting close to "fill out this form," which is what we're trying to avoid.

**Tension: Agent autonomy vs. guardrails.** The whole system assumes agents can't be trusted to plan well on their own. Maybe that's true today. But the system should be designed so that as agents get better at planning, the guardrails feel helpful rather than insulting. An agent that genuinely understands the codebase shouldn't have to prove it by filling out observation templates. The ideal system would detect that planning was done well and reduce the ceremony, not apply the same process regardless of quality.

**Tension: This might just not work for current models.** The deepest problem may be that current LLM agents don't actually do integrative thinking well, regardless of prompting structure. They pattern-match to what "good planning" looks like without the underlying cognition. If so, the honest answer is that the planning phase should be automated (using the existing `synthesize_epics()` LLM path with a very good prompt) rather than manual, and the multi-stage approach is solving a problem that can't be solved by process design. The best planning guardrail might be a good prompt to a strong model, not a workflow that guides a weak one.

### Recommendation

If I had to ship something:

1. **Enrich the observation surface** — make `plan synthesize` show findings grouped by file and highlight potential contradictions, not just a flat list. This costs very little and makes the right thing easier.

2. **Require structured observations with finding ID citations** — not prose reports with character minimums. The tool validates that most findings are referenced. This is a genuine guard against skimming.

3. **Store observations in the plan data model** — make them queryable during execution. Surface them in `next` cards so the agent's own planning work pays off visibly.

4. **Don't build five stages.** Build two: observe, then structure. The boundary is real and meaningful. More stages are overhead with diminishing returns.

5. **Invest in the automated synthesis path.** The `_SYNTHESIS_SYSTEM_PROMPT` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/epic_synthesis.py` is already good. Making it great (better contradiction detection, better dependency reasoning, better root-cause prompting) might produce more improvement than any amount of manual-planning process design.

The meta-lesson: **the best guardrails make the right thing feel natural.** Showing findings grouped by file with contradictions highlighted makes it natural to think about root causes. Requiring finding ID citations makes it natural to be specific. Surfacing planning notes during execution makes it natural to care about planning quality. These are all lower-ceremony than a five-stage process, and they work by shaping the cognitive environment rather than mandating behavior.

```
   178→
   179→    # Migrate epics → clusters (v3 → v4)
   180→    old_epics = plan.pop("epics", None)
   181→    if old_epics and isinstance(old_epics, dict):
   182→        clusters = plan["clusters"]
   183→        now = utc_now()
   184→        for name, epic in old_epics.items():
   185→            if name not in clusters:
   186→                clusters[name] = {
   187→                    "name": name,
   188→                    "description": epic.get("thesis"),
   189→                    "finding_ids": list(epic.get("finding_ids", [])),
   190→                    "auto": True,
   191→                    "cluster_key": f"epic::{name}",
   192→                    "action": f"desloppify plan focus {name}",
   193→                    "user_modified": False,
   194→                    "created_at": epic.get("created_at", now),
   195→                    "updated_at": epic.get("updated_at", now),
   196→                    "thesis": epic.get("thesis"),
   197→                    "direction": epic.get("direction"),
   198→                    "root_cause": epic.get("root_cause"),
   199→                    "agent_safe": epic.get("agent_safe", False),
   200→                    "dependency_order": epic.get("dependency_order", 999),
   201→                    "action_steps": epic.get("action_steps", []),
   202→                    "status": epic.get("status", "pending"),
   203→                    "synthesis_version": epic.get("synthesis_version", 0),
   204→                    "supersedes": epic.get("supersedes", []),
   205→                    "dismissed": epic.get("dismissed", []),
   206→                    "source_clusters": epic.get("source_clusters", []),
   207→                }
   208→
   209→
   210→def synthesis_clusters(plan: PlanModel) -> dict[str, Cluster]:
   211→    """Return clusters that were created by epic synthesis (have a thesis)."""
   212→    return {
   213→        name: cluster
   214→        for name, cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).items()
   215→        if cluster.get("thesis")
   216→    }
   217→
   218→
   219→def validate_plan(plan: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   220→    """Raise ValueError when plan invariants are violated."""
   221→    if not isinstance(plan.get("version"), int):
   222→        raise ValueError("plan.version must be an int")
   223→    if not isinstance(plan.get("queue_order"), list):
   224→        raise ValueError("plan.queue_order must be a list")
   225→
   226→    # No ID should appear in both queue_order and skipped
   227→    skipped_ids = set(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
   228→    overlap = set(plan["queue_order"]) & skipped_ids
   229→    if overlap:
   230→        raise ValueError(
   231→            f"IDs cannot appear in both queue_order and skipped: {sorted(overlap)}"
   232→        )
   233→
   234→    # Validate skip entry kinds
   235→    for fid, entry in plan.get("skipped", {}).items():
   236→        kind = entry.get("kind")
   237→        if kind not in VALID_SKIP_KINDS:
   238→            raise ValueError(
   239→                f"Invalid skip kind {kind!r} for {fid}; must be one of {sorted(VALID_SKIP_KINDS)}"
   240→            )
   241→
   242→
   243→__all__ = [
   244→    "EPIC_PREFIX",
   245→    "EpicSynthesisMeta",
   246→    "PLAN_VERSION",
   247→    "Cluster",
   248→    "ItemOverride",
   249→    "PlanModel",
   250→    "SkipEntry",
   251→    "SupersededEntry",
   252→    "VALID_EPIC_DIRECTIONS",
   253→    "VALID_SKIP_KINDS",
   254→    "empty_plan",
   255→    "ensure_plan_defaults",
   256→    "synthesis_clusters",
   257→    "validate_plan",
   258→]
   259→
```

```
1→"""Epic synthesis engine — LLM-driven meta-plan for review findings.
     2→
     3→Clusters review findings by root cause into epics, dismisses false positives,
     4→resolves contradictions, and orders by dependency. The synthesis is incremental:
     5→it updates existing epics rather than recreating from scratch.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from __future__ import annotations
     9→
    10→import json
    11→import logging
    12→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
    13→from typing import Any
    14→
    15→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import (
    16→    EPIC_PREFIX,
    17→    VALID_EPIC_DIRECTIONS,
    18→    Cluster,
    19→    PlanModel,
    20→    ensure_plan_defaults,
    21→    synthesis_clusters,
    22→)
    23→from desloppify.engine._plan.stale_dimensions import review_finding_snapshot_hash
    24→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel, utc_now
    25→
```
